Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have a clear picture:

1. **Training data**: `ultrachat_8k` is from `train_sft` split of `ultrachat_200k` dataset (first 8000 examples from ~207k). The dataset_idx values should range from 0 to ~7999.

2. **Eval sample (existing)**: `ultrachat_eval_sample.json` — 50 conversations with `source: "ultrachat_8k"` and `dataset_idx` values ranging from 35 to 7902. These are all within the range 0-8000, and the source explicitly says `ultrachat_8k`. 

So the existing eval data overlaps with training data—that's a data leakage problem.

3. **Heldout eval (new)**: `ultrachat_eval_heldout.json` — 50 conversations with `source: "ultrachat_200k_heldout"` and `dataset_idx` values from 8843 to 68199. These come from a completely separate partition, which is exactly what we need for proper held-out evaluation.

The worker created the right solution here. The critical issue is that the existing 4-way eval was run on contaminated data.

Now, the relative comparison between TG, UG, and LoRA-only might still hold even with the data leakage—all three methods trained and evaluated on the same data, so the relative ranking of their GAR scores should be valid even if the absolute values are inflated. But for a paper, held-out evaluation is non-negotiable, and I need to check whether that's been run yet.

I'm wondering if there's already a script set up to evaluate on the held-out data, and what exactly the worker accomplished beyond generating the data file itself.
```

exp_id: phase1_gar_diag\\n    role: evidence\\n    result: GAR衰减70.8%但归一化后仅~21%\\n  depends_on: []\\n- id: D004\\n  date: 2026-05-18 13:37 UTC\\n  summary: Kill v3, run v3b (λ=10, gate_lr=10x, init_bias=0.0) as GAR-driven gating的最终验证。500步内gate无分化则GAR→gate梯度路径判定失败。\\n  rationale: v1/v2/v3 三个版本全部无法驱动gate学习选择性干预：v1 epsilon过高(L_GAR=0)，v2 hinge退化平衡点，v3\\n    maximize模式梯度太弱(gate冻结在0.876±0.001)。根因一致：GAR→attention→hidden→gate间接梯度路径太弱。v3b同时最大化三个信号强度维度(λ=10,\\n    gate_lr=10x, init_bias=0.0给sigmoid最大梯度区间)，如果仍然失败则排除\\\"信号不够强\\\"的解释，确认是梯度路径结构性问题。\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Turn distribution comparison:

**Heldout eval**: 50 conversations
- 5 turns: ~30 (60%)
- 6 turns: ~10 (20%)
- 7 turns: ~10 (20%)
- Range: 5-7 turns

**Eval sample (existing)**: 50 conversations
- 5 turns: ~20 (40%)
- 6 turns: ~15 (30%)
- 7 turns: ~15 (30%)
- Range: 5-7 turns

Both datasets have conversations with 5-7 turns. The heldout set is slightly skewed toward shorter conversations (more 5-turn), but the difference isn't huge.


The original Phase 1 diagnostic used 10-turn conversations and showed GAR decay, but the eval data here maxes out at 7 turns—notably shorter. Since both the existing and heldout sets use the same range, the comparison between them should still be valid for this task, even if they don't capture the longer conversation patterns from the initial study. The heldout set aligns with typical UltraChat lengths, so it's suitable for data prep purposes.

Looking at the worker metadata, the timestamp in the task name indicates it was dispatched around 13:37 UTC on May 19, and since this is just data preparation rather than an experiment, there's no registry entry to check. I should look for any scripts or updates related to the heldout evaluation setup.
```
